````
- path: AGENTS.md
  content: |
      # AI Motion Design Studio — Agent Playbook
      
      This folder is a one-place studio for generating motion design videos with AI.
      You (the AI agent) run the whole pipeline here: idea → script → voiceover →
      storyboard → design → Remotion scenes → rendered MP4. The human only makes
      decisions; you do the work with the tools below. **Never ask the human to
      explain setup, install things, or write code.**
      
      ## Golden rules
      
      1. **Never reinstall.** The Remotion template at `templates/remotion-vertical`
         has `node_modules` and agent skills already installed. Projects link to that
         `node_modules` automatically. Do not run `npm install` in a project unless
         `video new` tells you it fell back to installing.
      2. **Never write plumbing code.** Every repetitive operation has a command
         (below). If you catch yourself writing boilerplate (timeline math, audio
         concatenation, project scaffolding, theme copying) — use the CLI instead.
      3. **Nothing ships without approval.** Script, audio, storyboard, and design
         each get explicit human approval before moving to the next phase. Record
         approvals in `projects/<name>/project.json` (`scriptApproved`,
         `audioApproved`, `storyboardApproved`, `designApproved`).
      4. **One phase at a time.** Don't storyboard before audio is approved. Don't
         build scenes before the storyboard is approved. Timing comes from audio, so
         audio before visuals, always.
      5. **Keep the human in the loop with playback, not descriptions.** When audio
         or a preview is ready, play it (`video play`, `video dev`) and wait for a
         verdict. Never ask "does that sound right?" without letting them hear it.
      
      ## The one command
      
      ```
      node tools/video.mjs <command>          # from the repo root
      ```
      
      | Command | Purpose |
      |---|---|
      | `doctor` | Verify environment (node, ffmpeg, uv, kokoro models, template) |
      | `new <name> --preset reel\|youtube\|youtube-short\|square\|custom [--duration <sec>] [--style <style>]` | Scaffold a project |
      | `list` / `status <name>` | See projects and their phase |
      | `tts <name> [--only L2,L5] [--voice <id>] [--speed <x>] [--play]` | Synthesize voiceover from `script.json` |
      | `play <name> [--line L2]` | Play the full voiceover or one line |
      | `sync <name>` | Regenerate `timeline.ts` from audio (also runs after `tts`) |
      | `style` / `style <name> --set <style>` | List/apply design styles |
      | `dev <name>` | Start Remotion Studio (live preview) |
      | `render <name> [--quality preview\|final]` | Render MP4 to `projects/<name>/output/` |
      | `delete <name>` | Delete a project |
      
      ## The workflow
      
      Follow `docs/workflow.md` in order. Summary:
      
      1. **Intake** — Ask: what's the idea? Which frame (`reel` 1080×1920,
         `youtube` 1920×1080, `youtube-short`, `square`, or custom)? Rough duration?
         Then `video new <name> --preset <frame> --duration <sec>`.
      2. **Script** — Ask if they have a prewritten script.
         - *Have one:* split it into short spoken **lines** (one sentence or phrase
           each) into `projects/<name>/script.json` (`lines: [{id: "L1", text: "..."}]`).
         - *Don't:* generate one from the idea, present it, iterate.
         Loops: edit a line → `video tts <name> --only L1` re-synthesizes just that
         line. Hate all of it → delete `script.json` lines and regenerate.
         Mark `scriptApproved` when the human is happy with the words.
      3. **Voice** — `video tts <name> --play`. Let them hear it. If a line is off,
         edit its text and `--only` regen; if the voice itself is wrong, try another
         Kokoro voice (`uv run --project voice-lab python voice-lab/tts.py voices`).
         Mark `audioApproved` only when they confirm the whole track.
      4. **Storyboard** — Write `projects/<name>/storyboard.md`: one section per
         audio line, VERY detailed (visuals, layout, motion, text on screen, which
         frames). Human can edit manually or ask you to rewrite parts/whole. Mark
         `storyboardApproved` when confirmed.
      5. **Design** — Discuss feel: mood, colors, how many fonts, animation density,
         transitions. Either map to an existing style
         (`node tools/video.mjs style` lists them; each style folder has a README
         with the full design language) or create a new style in
         `video-design-types/` (see `video-design-types/README.md`). Apply with
         `video style <name> --set <style>`. Mark `designApproved`.
      6. **Build** — Create one scene component per storyboard section in
         `projects/<name>/video/src/scenes/`, register them in `scenes/index.ts`
         keyed by line id (`L1`, `L2`, …). Read `docs/remotion-guide.md` first.
         Preview with `video dev <name>`.
      7. **Render** — Only when explicitly asked. `video render <name>`.
      
      ## Project anatomy
      
      ```
      projects/<name>/
        project.json      # phase, frame, fps, style, voice, approvals — edit to record progress
        script.json       # {voice, speed, lines: [{id, text}]} — the source of truth for TTS
        audio/            # per-line wavs + manifest.json + voiceover.wav (generated)
        storyboard.md     # approved visual plan, one section per line (you write, human edits)
        design.md         # design discussion outcome (mood, palette, fonts, motion)
        video/            # Remotion project (scaffolded from template)
          src/data/timeline.ts   # AUTO-GENERATED by `video sync` — never hand-edit
          src/data/theme.ts      # design tokens, written by `video style --set`
          src/scenes/            # you build these; registry in index.ts
          public/audio/          # audio copies for Remotion (generated)
        output/           # rendered MP4s
      ```
      
      ## Reference docs
      
      - `docs/workflow.md` — phase-by-phase instructions, questions to ask, loops
      - `docs/scripting.md` — writing TTS-friendly scripts (line splitting, pacing)
      - `docs/storyboarding.md` — storyboard format with a worked example
      - `docs/remotion-guide.md` — building scenes, THEME tokens, motion helpers
      - `docs/voice.md` — Kokoro voices, speed, troubleshooting
      - Skills (load when relevant): `skills/scriptwriting/`, `skills/voiceover/`,
        `skills/motion-design/`, `skills/rendering/`, `skills/research/`
      - Vendored Remotion skills: `templates/remotion-vertical/.agents/skills/`
      
- path: docs/workflow.md
  content: |
      # Workflow — Phase by Phase
      
      The pipeline is linear with approval gates. Each phase ends with the human
      saying yes before the next begins. Record approvals in
      `projects/<name>/project.json`.
      
      ```
      intake → script → voice → storyboard → design → build → render
         ▲        │        │         │           │
         └────────┴────────┴─────────┴───────────┘
              (edit loops live here — go back as needed)
      ```
      
      ---
      
      ## Phase 1 — Intake
      
      **Ask (short, in this order):**
      1. "What's the video about?" (the idea)
      2. "What frame? Instagram Reel / YouTube (16:9) / YouTube Short / Square /
         custom ratio?"
      3. "Roughly how long? (seconds)" — used as a target; the real duration comes
         from the audio.
      
      **Then:**
      ```
      node tools/video.mjs new <name> --preset reel --duration 30 [--style <style>]
      ```
      Pick a short lowercase-dash name. If they already know the look, pass
      `--style`; otherwise choose later.
      
      ---
      
      ## Phase 2 — Script
      
      **Ask:** "Do you have a prewritten script, or should I write one?"
      
      ### If they have a script
      Split it into **lines** — each line is one natural spoken sentence or phrase,
      short enough to be one visual beat (roughly 3–12 words). Write
      `projects/<name>/script.json`:
      
      ```json
      {
        "voice": "af_heart",
        "speed": 1.0,
        "lines": [
          { "id": "L1", "text": "Every great product starts with a stubborn idea." },
          { "id": "L2", "text": "Ours started in a folder like this one." }
        ]
      }
      ```
      
      Rules for splitting: split where a breath would go; keep each line a complete
      thought; stable ids (`L1`, `L2`, …) that you NEVER renumber later (audio files
      and scenes key off them).
      
      ### If they don't
      Generate 3–10 lines from the idea (see `docs/scripting.md` +
      `skills/scriptwriting/`), show them the numbered lines, and iterate.
      
      ### Edit loops (this is the core of the experience)
      
      | Human says | You do |
      |---|---|
      | "Line 3 is clunky, try X" | Edit line 3's text in `script.json`, run `video tts <name> --only L3 --play` |
      | "Too slow / too fast" | `video tts <name> --speed 1.1` (or edit `speed` in script.json) |
      | "Different voice" | `video tts <name> --voice am_michael --force --play` |
      | "I hate all of it" | Clear `lines: []` in `script.json`, regenerate a fresh script from scratch |
      | "Add a line at the end" | Append `L<n>` with next id, run `video tts <name>` (only the new line synthesizes) |
      
      **Approval:** when the words are right → set `scriptApproved: true` in
      `project.json`, move to voice.
      
      ---
      
      ## Phase 3 — Voice
      
      ```
      node tools/video.mjs tts <name> --play
      ```
      This synthesizes every line, concatenates `voiceover.wav`, copies audio into
      the Remotion project, and regenerates `timeline.ts` — so audio and visuals can
      never drift. The human listens.
      
      - Per-line fixes: same `--only` loop as above.
      - Whole-track re-approval: after ANY text change, the affected line re-renders
        automatically; play the full track again before approving.
      - Voice catalog: `uv run --project voice-lab python voice-lab/tts.py voices`
        (54 voices; `af_*`/`am_*` = American, `bf_*`/`bm_*` = British).
        Detail in `docs/voice.md`.
      
      **Approval:** `audioApproved: true`. Do not storyboard against audio you may
      still change — timings would shift.
      
      ---
      
      ## Phase 4 — Storyboard
      
      Read `docs/storyboarding.md`, then write `projects/<name>/storyboard.md` with
      one section per audio line. Each section is a VERY detailed paragraph
      (visuals, layout, on-screen text, motion, enter/exit, style references) —
      detailed enough that a scene can be coded from it alone.
      
      Present it; offer both edit paths:
      - **Manual:** "Edit `storyboard.md` directly and tell me when you're done."
      - **AI:** "Rewrite section L3", "make L2's animation calmer", "redo the whole
        storyboard with X feel."
      
      **Approval:** `storyboardApproved: true`. Only now does design/build start.
      
      ---
      
      ## Phase 5 — Design
      
      Discuss feel with questions like: mood (calm / punchy / premium / playful)?
      How many fonts (1 display + 1 body is the sweet spot)? Color: one accent or a
      full palette? Animation density (few slow moves vs. constant motion)? Grain,
      gradients, borders?
      
      **Two paths:**
      - **Existing style:** `node tools/video.mjs style` lists them; read the
        style's README for the exact language (palette table, typography rules,
        motion vocabulary, example scenes). Apply:
        `node tools/video.mjs style <name> --set cinematic`.
      - **New style:** create `video-design-types/<style>/` with `style.json`,
        `tokens.ts`, `README.md` — see `video-design-types/README.md` for the
        contract. Then apply it like any other style.
      
      Record decisions in `design.md` (palette, fonts + how many, motion density,
      transitions, references). **Approval:** `designApproved: true`.
      
      ---
      
      ## Phase 6 — Build scenes
      
      Read `docs/remotion-guide.md`. For each storyboard section, create a component
      in `video/src/scenes/` and register it in `scenes/index.ts`:
      
      ```tsx
      // video/src/scenes/L1Hook.tsx
      export const L1Hook: React.FC<SceneProps> = ({ line }) => { ... }
      ```
      ```ts
      // scenes/index.ts
      export const SCENES = { L1: L1Hook, L2: L2Stat, ... };
      ```
      
      Preview continuously with `node tools/video.mjs dev <name>` — Studio runs on
      the template's existing install, never reinstall. Unregistered lines fall back
      to `DefaultScene`, so the video always plays while you build.
      
      ---
      
      ## Phase 7 — Render
      
      Only when explicitly asked ("render it", "give me the MP4"):
      ```
      node tools/video.mjs render <name>                  # final quality
      node tools/video.mjs render <name> --quality preview  # half-scale, fast
      ```
      Output: `projects/<name>/output/<name>.mp4`.
      
      ---
      
      ## Managing projects
      
      - `video list` — all projects with phase/frame/audio/style.
      - `video status <name>` — full state + what's next.
      - Start a new project anytime: `video new <other>` — projects are independent
        folders; nothing global changes.
      - `video delete <name>` — removes the whole project (asks for confirmation
        unless `--yes`).
      - Resume a project: `video status <name>` shows exactly where the phase
        machine stands.
````

````
- path: docs/scripting.md
  content: |
      # Scripting for Voiceover
      
      Scripts are not essays — they are **sequences of spoken lines**, each line one
      visual beat. The audio drives everything downstream (scene timing, storyboard
      sections, animation pacing), so script quality decides video quality.
      
      ## File format — `projects/<name>/script.json`
      
      ```json
      {
        "voice": "af_heart",
        "speed": 1.0,
        "lines": [
          { "id": "L1", "text": "Most teams start with a blank page." },
          { "id": "L2", "text": "We start with a plan." }
        ]
      }
      ```
      
      - `id` — stable forever: `"L1"`, `"L2"`, … Audio files, timeline entries and
        scenes all key off it. **Never renumber**; insert new lines with the next
        free id (append at the end or give an id like `L2b` when inserting).
      - `text` — one sentence or phrase. Synthesized independently.
      - `voice` / `speed` — defaults; `video tts --voice/--speed` overrides.
      
      ## Line splitting rules
      
      1. **3–12 words per line.** Under 3 reads choppy; over ~15 words becomes two
         visual ideas glued together.
      2. **Split at breath points** — after a complete clause, where you'd pause
         reading aloud.
      3. **Each line = one visual beat.** Before writing a line, picture its scene.
         If you can't, the line is doing too much; split it.
      4. **Punchlines stand alone.** Put the payoff in its own line — it gets its
         own scene, its own pause.
      5. **Read it aloud.** If you stumble, rewrite. TTS will stumble too.
      
      ## Writing for Kokoro
      
      Kokoro is good but literal — it doesn't improvise.
      
      - Write numbers the way they should sound: `30%` fine, but "a third of their
        week" if you want it spoken that way. Spell out what matters:
        `"3 in 5"` vs `"three in five"`.
      - Avoid unexplained acronyms; add articles: `"an API"` not `"API"` alone.
      - Em-dashes and ellipses create unnatural jumps mid-line — end the line instead.
      - Line-final punctuation matters: end every line with `.` `?` or `!` so the
        prosody closes properly.
      - All-caps or unusual casing gets read oddly; normal sentence case.
      
      ## Pacing to duration
      
      Roughly **2.5–3.5 seconds per line** at speed 1.0 (varies with length). To hit
      a target duration:
      
      - 15s reel ≈ 5–6 lines
      - 30s reel ≈ 10–12 lines
      - 60s ≈ 20–24 lines
      
      Check reality after synthesis: `video tts` prints each line's duration and the
      total. Trim lines (not words mid-sentence) to shorten; add lines to lengthen.
      `speed` (0.8–1.3 sensible range) is a last-resort nudge, not a fix for too
      much copy.
      
      ## Edit-loop etiquette
      
      - Change text → `video tts <name> --only <ids>` (cached lines aren't redone).
      - Changing any line's text changes total duration → `sync` runs automatically
        inside `tts`; the timeline re-flows. Storyboard sections referencing other
        lines don't need changes.
      - Re-approve: after edits, `scriptApproved` reflects the last agreed words —
        flip it back to `false` while reworking, `true` when agreed again.
      
      ## When the human has no script
      
      Generate one (skills: `skills/scriptwriting/`):
      
      1. Ask: who's the audience, what's the one takeaway, tone (hype / calm /
         educational / funny), CTA if any.
      2. Write a first draft as 5–10 lines using the rules above.
      3. Show it numbered (`L1 …`), ask for changes by line.
      4. Iterate until they approve → `scriptApproved: true`.
      
      Keep the "trash it all" path visible: they can always say "start over with
      X" — regenerate the whole `lines` array in one go, never patch line-by-line
      into a script they've rejected wholesale.
      
- path: docs/storyboarding.md
  content: |
      # Storyboarding
      
      `projects/<name>/storyboard.md` is the visual contract: one section per audio
      line, written so a scene can be built from it **alone** — no guessing. The
      human edits it manually or asks you to rewrite parts; scenes are only built
      once `storyboardApproved` is true.
      
      ## Format
      
      ```markdown
      # Storyboard — <name>
      
      ## L1 — "Most teams start with a blank page."
      
      **Visual:** ...detailed paragraph...
      **Motion:** ...detailed paragraph...
      **On-screen text:** ...
      **Style notes:** ...
      ```
      
      ## What "VERY detailed" means
      
      Each section must answer everything a coder needs:
      
      - **Visual** — layout (what sits where on the frame), every element (shapes,
        cards, text, numbers, images), hierarchy (what's biggest), and the
        composition rule being used (center hero, lower-third, left-aligned column…).
      - **Motion** — how each element enters (fade-up, stamp, slide, scale), timing
        in frames relative to line start (e.g. "title fades up frames 0–20, stat
        counts 20–70, everything exits 95–104"), stagger between elements, and the
        exit. Respect the style's motion vocabulary (from the style README) —
        a cinematic scene never SLAMS; a kinetic scene never gently fades.
      - **On-screen text** — exact strings, sizes relative to frame, weight/color
        from THEME (e.g. "accent color for `30%`").
      - **Style notes** — which THEME tokens matter here (accent block, gradient
        background, gold rule…), and any per-scene deviation the design phase blessed.
      - **Audio cue** — which word/beat the emphasis lands on, when the line's key
        moment is ("punch lands on 'blank'").
      
      Sections must cover **every** line in `script.json` (ids must match), in order.
      
      ## Worked example
      
      ```markdown
      ## L2 — "We start with a plan."
      
      **Visual:** Full-bleed background in `background`. A single centered column:
      a small uppercase label "OUR PROCESS" in `textSecondary`, 20px, letter-spaced,
      sits 180px above a large heading "A PLAN." in Impact at 160px, `text` white.
      Below it, a thick `accent` block (420×18px) will swipe across to underline the
      word. Behind everything, a giant outlined word "PLAN" at 10% opacity, 340px,
      slowly drifting left for depth.
      
      **Motion:** Line starts at frame 0 of this scene. Label fades up 16px over
      frames 0–14. "A" stamps in (scale 1.15→1.0 spring) frames 8–20; "PLAN." slams
      in word after it, frames 14–26, with the accent block swiping left→right
      frames 26–38 exactly on the narrator's stress of "plan". The watermark drifts
      −40px horizontally across the whole scene. Exit: all content slides up 60px and
      fades over the last 10 frames.
      
      **On-screen text:** "OUR PROCESS" (label), "A PLAN." (hero).
      
      **Style notes:** kinetic-typography — yellow accent used only on the underline
      block (black text stays white); hard offset shadow on "A PLAN.".
      
      **Audio cue:** emphasis on the word "plan" — the underline swipe must land
      when it's spoken (~60% through the line).
      ```
      
      ## Edit loops
      
      | Human says | You do |
      |---|---|
      | "Edit L4's paragraph myself" | Leave the file alone; they edit `storyboard.md`; re-read it before building |
      | "Rewrite just L4, calmer" | Rewrite only that section in place; show the diff summary |
      | "The whole thing feels off — try X mood" | Regenerate all sections around X; keep ids |
      | "Add a line" | Add `L<n>` to `script.json`, run `video tts --only L<n>`, add its section |
      
      After ANY script change post-approval, flip `storyboardApproved` to `false`
      until the affected sections are re-agreed.
      
      ## Approval
      
      Ask: "Storyboard ready? You can edit `storyboard.md` directly, or tell me what
      to change." Only `storyboardApproved: true` unlocks scene building.
      
- path: docs/remotion-guide.md
  content: |
      # Remotion Guide — Building Scenes
      
      Prerequisite reading: `templates/remotion-vertical/.agents/skills/remotion-best-practices/`
      is the vendored Remotion skill set (markup, multimedia, captions, rendering…).
      Load the relevant one when doing anything beyond simple scene components.
      **Never reinstall skills — they're already in the template and copied into
      every project by `video new`.**
      
      ## Project shape (per project)
      
      ```
      video/
        src/
          index.ts            # registerRoot — untouched
          Root.tsx            # Composition "Main", sizes from timeline data
          Video.tsx           # plays voiceover + lays scenes onto Sequence slots
          data/timeline.ts    # AUTO-GENERATED by `video sync` — never edit
          data/theme.ts       # THEME tokens — written by `video style --set`
          lib/motion.ts       # useFadeUp, usePunch, frames() helpers
          scenes/
            index.ts          # SCENES registry: { L1: Component, ... }
            DefaultScene.tsx  # fallback themed text scene
            L1Hook.tsx        # you build these, one per storyboard section
        public/audio/         # voiceover.wav + per-line wavs (copied by `tts`)
      ```
      
      ## The scene contract
      
      ```tsx
      import React from "react";
      import { AbsoluteFill } from "remotion";
      import { THEME } from "../data/theme";
      import { useFadeUp, usePunch, frames } from "../lib/motion";
      import type { SceneProps } from "./index";
      
      export const L1Hook: React.FC<SceneProps> = ({ line }) => {
        const { fps } = useVideoConfig();
        const enter = useFadeUp({ delay: 5 });
        const scale = usePunch({ delay: 12 });
      
        return (
          <AbsoluteFill style={{ /* background from THEME */ }}>
            ...
          </AbsoluteFill>
        );
      };
      ```
      
      - `line` = `{ id, text, audio, startFrame, durationFrames }` — the spoken
        text and your scene's exact length. **Frames inside a scene are local**: 0 =
        scene start (= line start), last frame = `durationFrames - 1`.
      - Register: `export const SCENES = { L1: L1Hook };` in `scenes/index.ts`.
        Unregistered lines get `DefaultScene`, so previews never break.
      
      ## Non-negotiable rules
      
      1. **No hardcoded colors/fonts/margins** — everything from `THEME`. That's
         what makes `video style --set` restyle the whole video instantly.
      2. **No unregistered `<Audio>`** — the voiceover plays once in `Video.tsx`.
         A second `<Audio>` double-plays and drifts.
      3. **Never edit `data/timeline.ts`** — regenerated by `video sync`.
         Need different timing? Change the audio, then re-sync.
      4. **Stay within your Sequence** — your component only exists for
         `durationFrames`; don't build cross-scene effects with absolute frames
         unless you know what you're doing (use `useCurrentFrame()` local frames).
      5. **Scene must read at a glance** — one idea per scene, per the storyboard.
      
      ## Timing math
      
      ```tsx
      import { useVideoConfig, useCurrentFrame, interpolate } from "remotion";
      import { frames } from "../lib/motion";
      
      const { fps } = useVideoConfig();               // project fps (30)
      const local = useCurrentFrame();                // 0 at scene start
      const t = frames(THEME.motion.durations.enter, fps); // theme frames → project frames
      ```
      Theme durations are authored at 30fps; `frames()` scales them if the project
      fps differs. Count-ups, staggers and exits should all be expressed relative to
      `durationFrames` so a scene fits no matter how long the line is.
      
      ## Animation helpers (`lib/motion.ts`)
      
      - `useFadeUp({ delay?, distance?, duration? })` → `{ opacity, transform }`
        style to spread. The everyday entrance.
      - `usePunch({ delay?, from?, to? })` → scale number for stamps/emphasis
        (spring with the theme's overshoot settings).
      - Combine with `interpolate` for counts/swipes; use `THEME.motion.easing` via
        `Easing.bezier(...)` for custom curves.
      
      ## What belongs in a scene vs. the style
      
      - Style decides palette/type/motion character — via `THEME`.
      - Storyboard decides what this line's scene IS.
      - You code the scene to both. If you need a new global visual (grain overlay,
        letterbox), put it in `Video.tsx` once, guarded by a `THEME.treatment` flag —
        not per-scene.
      
      ## Preview & iterate
      
      ```
      node tools/video.mjs dev <name>     # Remotion Studio on the shared install
      ```
      Studio's timeline lets the human scrub any line. Keep it open while building;
      after each scene, jump to its frames and ask for a verdict. Remotion skill
      refs: `remotion-create` (layouts), `remotion-markup` (composition structure),
      `remotion-captions` (if adding captions).
      
      ## Render
      
      `node tools/video.mjs render <name>` → `projects/<name>/output/<name>.mp4`
      (`--quality preview` for half-scale speed). Render only when asked.
      
- path: docs/voice.md
  content: |
      # Voice — Kokoro TTS
      
      Voiceover runs locally through Kokoro (ONNX) in `voice-lab/`. No API keys, no
      network, no installs — models are already in `voice-lab/models/` and the
      environment is `voice-lab/.venv` managed by `uv`.
      
      ## Everyday commands
      
      ```bash
      node tools/video.mjs tts <name>                  # full track (incremental)
      node tools/video.mjs tts <name> --only L3,L4     # regen just these lines
      node tools/video.mjs tts <name> --force          # regenerate everything
      node tools/video.mjs tts <name> --voice bm_george --speed 1.1 --play
      node tools/video.mjs play <name>                 # full track
      node tools/video.mjs play <name> --line L3       # single line
      ```
      
      Under the hood (only if you need the raw tool):
      
      ```bash
      uv run --project voice-lab python voice-lab/tts.py voices
      uv run --project voice-lab python voice-lab/tts.py batch --script <file> --outdir <dir> [--only L2]
      uv run --project voice-lab python voice-lab/tts.py concat --outdir <dir> --out <file>
      uv run --project voice-lab python voice-lab/tts.py line --text "..." --out one.wav
      ```
      
      ## Voices (54)
      
      Naming: prefix letter = gender (`a` female, `b` male), second = accent
      (`f`/`m` same as first is conventional; `f` = female, `m` = male), suffix =
      region (`us`, `gb`). Examples:
      
      | Voice | Feel |
      |---|---|
      | `af_heart` | Warm, friendly — default |
      | `af_bella` | Bright, expressive |
      | `af_nicole` | Soft, breathy |
      | `am_michael` | Confident male, good for narration |
      | `bm_george` | British male, documentary |
      | `bf_isabella` | British female, polished |
      
      Full list: `node tools/video.mjs` → `uv run --project voice-lab python voice-lab/tts.py voices`.
      Trial a candidate on one line before switching the whole track:
      `--voice <id> --only L1 --play`, then `--force` once they like it.
      
      ## Speed
      
      `--speed` (or `speed` in `script.json`), sensible range **0.8–1.3**. Use it to
      fine-tune pacing, not to cram extra copy — cut words instead.
      
      ## What the pipeline produces
      
      `video tts <name>` →
      - `projects/<name>/audio/L*.wav` — per-line files (the edit unit)
      - `projects/<name>/audio/manifest.json` — per-line `duration` + `start`
        (computed with a 0.25s gap between lines)
      - `projects/<name>/audio/voiceover.wav` — concatenated track
      - copies into `video/public/audio/` + regenerated `timeline.ts` (sync)
      
      **Caching:** each line is hashed by `voice|speed|text`. Unchanged lines are
      never re-synthesized — that's why `--only` and plain re-runs are fast after
      the first pass (first pass: ~0.5–1s per line).
      
      ## Troubleshooting
      
      - *"uv: command not found"* → install uv (docs: docs.astral.sh/uv), then
        re-run `node tools/video.mjs doctor`.
      - *Models missing* → `voice-lab/models/` must contain
        `kokoro-v1.0.int8.onnx` + `voices-v1.0.bin` (already present in this setup).
      - *No player* → `video play` tries ffplay, paplay, aplay, mpv, afplay; install
        any one (ffmpeg covers ffplay).
      - *Garbled pronunciation* → rewrite the word phonetically in the line text
        (see `docs/scripting.md`).
      - *Total duration off target* → change the number of lines / trim copy;
        durations are printed by every `tts` run.
````

````
          // Long, filmic deceleration.
          easing: [0.22, 1, 0.36, 1] as const,
          durations: { enter: 45, exit: 30, hold: 55 }, // slow!
          stagger: 8,
          spring: { damping: 200, stiffness: 60, mass: 1.4 },

          border: "none",
          radius: 4,
          grain: true,
          gradient:
            "radial-gradient(ellipse at 50% 40%, rgba(201,162,39,0.08) 0%, transparent 60%), linear-gradient(180deg, #0A0A0F 0%, #101018 50%, #0A0A0F 100%)",

      } as const;

- path: projects/engineering-student/video/src/lib/motion.ts
  content: |
      // Motion helpers — thin wrappers around Remotion primitives that apply the
      // active THEME's easing/durations, so scenes stay short and consistent.
      //
      //   const style = useFadeUp({ delay: 10 });
      //   const scale = usePunch();
      
      import { Easing, interpolate, spring, useCurrentFrame, useVideoConfig } from "remotion";
      import { THEME } from "../data/theme";
      
      /** THEME easing as a mutable tuple for Easing.bezier(). */
      const EASE = THEME.motion.easing as unknown as [number, number, number, number];
      const EASING = Easing.bezier(...EASE);
      
      /** Scale a theme duration (defined @30fps) to the project fps. */
      export const frames = (n: number, fps: number): number => Math.round((n * fps) / 30);
      
      type FadeUpOpts = {
        /** Frames after scene start before beginning. */
        delay?: number;
        /** Initial vertical offset in px. */
        distance?: number;
        /** Enter duration in frames (theme default if omitted). */
        duration?: number;
      };
      
      /** Fade + rise entrance. Returns a style object to spread onto the element. */
      export const useFadeUp = (opts: FadeUpOpts = {}) => {
        const frame = useCurrentFrame();
        const { fps } = useVideoConfig();
        const { delay = 0, distance = 28 } = opts;
        const duration = opts.duration ?? THEME.motion.durations.enter;
      
        const progress = interpolate(frame, [delay, delay + frames(duration, fps)], [0, 1], {
          extrapolateLeft: "clamp",
          extrapolateRight: "clamp",
          easing: EASING,
        });
      
        return {
          opacity: progress,
          transform: `translateY(${(1 - progress) * distance}px)`,
        };
      };
      
      /**
       * Spring punch: overshoots then settles. Good for emphasis (scale punch, stamps).
       * Returns a 0→to scale factor to apply via transform.
       */
      export const usePunch = (opts: { delay?: number; to?: number; from?: number } = {}) => {
        const frame = useCurrentFrame();
        const { fps } = useVideoConfig();
        const { delay = 0, to = 1, from = 0.82 } = opts;
      
        const s = spring({
          frame: frame - delay,
          fps,
          config: {
            damping: THEME.motion.spring.damping,
            stiffness: THEME.motion.spring.stiffness,
            mass: THEME.motion.spring.mass,
          },
          durationInFrames: frames(THEME.motion.durations.enter, fps),
        });
      
        return interpolate(s, [0, 1], [from, to]);
      };
      
- path: projects/engineering-student/video/src/Video.tsx
  content: |
      import React from "react";
      import { AbsoluteFill, Audio, Sequence, staticFile } from "remotion";
      import { HAS_AUDIO, LINES } from "./data/timeline";
      import { THEME } from "./data/theme";
      import { getScene } from "./scenes";
      
      /**
       * Video — the root composition body.
       *
       * Plays the full voiceover and lays every timeline line's scene into its
       * audio slot. Scenes are pure visuals; timing comes from the timeline data
       * written by `video sync`, so audio and visuals never drift.
       */
      export const Video: React.FC = () => {
        const background = THEME.treatment.gradient
          ? THEME.treatment.gradient
          : THEME.colors.background;
      
        return (
          <AbsoluteFill style={{ backgroundColor: THEME.colors.background }}>
            <AbsoluteFill style={{ background }} />
      
            {HAS_AUDIO && <Audio src={staticFile("audio/voiceover.wav")} />}
      
            {LINES.map((line) => {
              const Scene = getScene(line.id);
              return (
                <Sequence
                  key={line.id}
                  from={line.startFrame}
                  durationInFrames={line.durationFrames}
                  name={line.id}
                >
                  <Scene line={line} />
                </Sequence>
              );
            })}
      
            {/* Pre-audio placeholder so the template renders something meaningful */}
            {!HAS_AUDIO && LINES.length === 0 && (
              <AbsoluteFill
                style={{
                  justifyContent: "center",
                  alignItems: "center",
                  fontFamily: THEME.fonts.body,
                  color: THEME.colors.textSecondary,
                  fontSize: 36,
                }}
              >
                No audio yet — run the voice phase.
              </AbsoluteFill>
            )}
          </AbsoluteFill>
        );
      };
      
- path: projects/engineering-student/video/src/scenes/index.ts
  content: |
      // Scene registry — maps audio line ids to the component that visualizes them.
      //
      // The agent builds one scene component per storyboard section and registers it
      // here. Lines without a registered scene fall back to DefaultScene (styled
      // line text), so the video always renders, even mid-build.
      //
      //   import { HookScene } from "./HookScene";
      //   export const SCENES = { L1: HookScene, L2: HookScene, L3: StatScene };
      
      import type React from "react";
      import type { TimelineLine } from "../data/timeline";
      import { DefaultScene } from "./DefaultScene";
      
      export type SceneProps = {
        line: TimelineLine;
      };
      
      export const SCENES: Record<string, React.FC<SceneProps>> = {
        // L1: HookScene,
      };
      
      export const getScene = (lineId: string): React.FC<SceneProps> =>
        SCENES[lineId] ?? DefaultScene;
      
- path: projects/engineering-student/video/src/scenes/DefaultScene.tsx
  content: |
      import React from "react";
      import { AbsoluteFill } from "remotion";
      import { THEME } from "../data/theme";
      import { useFadeUp } from "../lib/motion";
      import type { SceneProps } from "./index";
      
      /**
       * DefaultScene — fallback when a line has no custom scene yet.
       * Shows the spoken line as themed text, so previews work from day one.
       */
      const ALIGN: Record<string, "center" | "flex-start"> = {
        center: "center",
        left: "flex-start",
      };
      
      export const DefaultScene: React.FC<SceneProps> = ({ line }) => {
        const enter = useFadeUp();
      
        return (
          <AbsoluteFill
            style={{
              backgroundColor: THEME.colors.background,
              justifyContent: "center",
              alignItems: ALIGN[THEME.layout.align] ?? "center",
              padding: THEME.layout.safeMargin,
            }}
          >
            <p
              style={{
                ...enter,
                maxWidth: THEME.layout.maxTextWidth,
                margin: 0,
                color: THEME.colors.text,
                fontFamily: THEME.fonts.heading,
                fontWeight: THEME.fonts.headingWeight,
                fontSize: 72,
                lineHeight: THEME.fonts.lineHeights.heading,
                letterSpacing: THEME.fonts.headingLetterSpacing,
              }}
            >
              {line.text}
            </p>
          </AbsoluteFill>
        );
      };
      
- path: video-design-types/kinetic-typography/README.md
  content: |
      # Kinetic Typography
      
      Words ARE the visuals. Every audio line is performed by type — punched in,
      slammed, sliced, scaled. The viewer reads along without trying to. High
      contrast, high energy, zero quiet moments.
      
      ## Palette
      
      | Token | Value | Use |
      |---|---|---|
      | background | `#0D0D0D` | Near-black stage |
      | surface | `#171717` / `#232323` | Occasional blocks behind words |
      | text | `#FFFFFF` | Default word color |
      | accent | `#FFE600` | Hi-vis yellow — the keyword color |
      | accentAlt | `#FF3B30` | Red — reserve for a SINGLE word per scene max |
      
      Rules:
      - One keyword per line gets the accent. Flip its background block too (yellow bg, black text).
      - Background can flash to accent for 4–8 frames on impact moments (beats, punchlines).
      
      ## Typography
      
      - Display: Impact/Arial Black, weight 900, HUGE — words should nearly touch the frame edges.
      - Line-height 0.95: stacked lines nearly collide. That's the look.
      - No thin weights anywhere. Captions are bold sans at 28–36px.
      - Text can be rotated ±2°, scaled non-uniformly, or clipped for emphasis — but keep it readable.
      
      ## Motion
      
      - Words arrive on the beat: 8–12 frame entrances, spring with overshoot (`motion.spring`).
      - Stagger word-by-word at 2 frames — a sentence should cascade like a drum fill.
      - Exits are fast cuts or vertical slams (translateY off-frame), never fades.
      - Background number/word watermarks can scroll or pulse continuously.
      - Emphasis moves: scale punch (1.0 → 1.12 → 1.0 in 8 frames), color flip, block reveal.
      
      ## Scene composition
      
      - Text-first: if a scene has no type on screen, it's wrong.
      - Layer hierarchy: giant background word (8% opacity) + foreground spoken words + optional accent block.
      - Sync to the audio: each emphasis lands when the narrator stresses it. Read the line aloud, find the stressed word, that's your accent moment.
      - Keep every frame busy — even holds get a slow drift or pulse.
      
      ## Example scene shape
      
      ```
      [AUDIO LINE]: "Nobody reads the terms. Nobody."
      → "Nobody" SLAMS in (yellow block, black text), scale-punch on landing.
        "reads the" types on white, 2-frame stagger.
        "terms." stamps in + red underline swipe.
        Cut. "Nobody." re-enters bigger, red, fills the frame. Hold with pulse.
      ```
      
- path: video-design-types/kinetic-typography/tokens.ts

      // Design tokens — Kinetic Typography
      // Copied to <project>/video/src/data/theme.ts by `video style <name> --set kinetic-typography`.
      
      export const THEME = {
        id: "kinetic-typography",
        name: "Kinetic Typography",
      
        colors: {
          background: "#0D0D0D",
          surface: "#171717",
          surfaceAlt: "#232323",
          text: "#FFFFFF",
          textSecondary: "#A3A3A3",
          accent: "#FFE600", // hi-vis yellow
          accentAlt: "#FF3B30", // punch red for one-word emphasis
          accentSoft: "rgba(255, 230, 0, 0.15)",
          line: "rgba(255,255,255,0.14)",
        },
      
        fonts: {
          heading: "Impact, 'Arial Black', 'Helvetica Neue', sans-serif",
          body: "'Helvetica Neue', Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif",
          mono: "'SF Mono', Consolas, monospace",
          headingWeight: 900,
          bodyWeight: 700,
          headingLetterSpacing: "0em",
          lineHeights: { heading: 0.95, body: 1.25 },
        },
      
        motion: {
          // Snappy out-ease — instant arrival, no soft landing.
          easing: [0.19, 1, 0.22, 1] as const,
          durations: { enter: 10, exit: 8, hold: 25 },
          stagger: 2, // fast cascade
          spring: { damping: 14, stiffness: 220, mass: 0.6 }, // bouncy, punchy
          maxAnimationsPerScene: 5, // this style thrives on layered motion
        },
      
        layout: {
          safeMargin: 72,
          maxTextWidth: 936,
          align: "center" as const,
        },
      
        treatment: {
          shadow: "6px 6px 0 rgba(0,0,0,0.9)", // hard offset shadow, no blur
          border: "3px solid #FFFFFF",
          radius: 0,
          grain: false,
          gradient: false,
        },
      } as const;
      
      export type Theme = typeof THEME;
````

````
- path: video-design-types/README.md
  content: |
      # Video Design Types
      
      Pre-made design languages the AI can apply to any project with one command:
      
      ```
      node tools/video.mjs style <project> --set cinematic
      node tools/video.mjs style            # list all styles
      ```
      
      Applying a style copies its `tokens.ts` into the project as
      `video/src/data/theme.ts`, so every scene reads colors, fonts and motion
      settings from `THEME` instead of hardcoding them.
      
      ## Available styles
      
      | Style | Feel |
      |---|---|
      | `minimal-tech` | Clean, calm, Apple-keynote whitespace, one accent |
      | `cinematic` | Dark film-title mood, gold accents, slow reveals |
      | `kinetic-typography` | Words as visuals, hi-contrast, fast and punchy |
      
      ## Folder format
      
      Each style folder contains:
      
      - `style.json` — `{ id, name, description }` (shown by `video style`)
      - `tokens.ts` — exports `THEME` with sections: `colors`, `fonts`, `motion`, `layout`, `treatment`
      - `README.md` — human/AI-facing design guide: palette rules, typography rules,
        motion vocabulary, scene composition guidance, and an example scene breakdown
      
      ## Creating a new style with AI
      
      1. Copy the `_template` folder and name it (lowercase-with-dashes).
      2. Ask the AI: *"Create a design style called <name> for <mood/reference>.
         Fill style.json, tokens.ts and README.md following the conventions of the
         other styles."*
      3. The AI must keep the same `THEME` keys (`colors`, `fonts`, `motion`,
         `layout`, `treatment`) so scenes compile against any style.
      4. Verify: `node tools/video.mjs style` should list it.
      
      Rules for token files:
      - `tokens.ts` must only use plain data (strings/numbers/arrays/objects) — it is
        copied verbatim into projects.
      - Fonts must be system font stacks (no font files to download/install).
      - `motion.durations` are in frames at 30fps; scale if the project fps differs.
      - Never reference other styles' tokens; each folder is self-contained.

        },

        },

- path: projects/engineering-student/video/src/data/timeline.ts
  content: |
      // Timeline data — the single source of truth for video length and audio sync.
      //
      // In a real project this file is REGENERATED by `node tools/video.mjs sync <name>`
      // from the audio manifest (projects/<name>/audio/manifest.json). Until you run
      // the voice phase, this placeholder keeps the project compilable.
      
      export type TimelineLine = {
        id: string;
        text: string;
        /** Path relative to public/ */
        audio: string;
        startFrame: number;
        durationFrames: number;
      };
      
      /** False until voiceover exists; Video.tsx skips <Audio> when unset. */
      export const HAS_AUDIO = false;
      
      export const FPS = 30;
      export const WIDTH = 1080;
      export const HEIGHT = 1920;
      export const TOTAL_DURATION_IN_FRAMES = 150;
      
      export const LINES: TimelineLine[] = [];
      
- path: projects/engineering-student/video/src/Root.tsx
  content: |
      import React from "react";
      import { Composition } from "remotion";
      import {
        FPS,
        HEIGHT,
        TOTAL_DURATION_IN_FRAMES,
        WIDTH,
      } from "./data/timeline";
      import { Video } from "./Video";
      
      export const RemotionRoot: React.FC = () => {
        return (
          <Composition
            id="Main"
            component={Video}
            durationInFrames={TOTAL_DURATION_IN_FRAMES}
            fps={FPS}
            width={WIDTH}
            height={HEIGHT}
          />
        );
      };
````

> **5** His college brochure promised a future-proof career. · **6** The syllabus was last updated when a DVD drive felt futuristic. · **7** In twenty twenty-six, his professor is still teaching Java applets. · **8** Meanwhile, the AI he's not allowed to use just finished his assignment. · **9** In four seconds.
> **10** So Rohan opens YouTube for one tutorial. · **11** His hostel Wi-Fi has a curfew. His ambition doesn't. · **12** Forty-seven tabs later, he's learning web development, machine learning, cybersecurity, and quantum computing. · **13** It's still Tuesday.
> **14** And every video starts the same way. · **15** You're already behind. · **16** So he builds a roadmap. Then a better roadmap. · **17** Then a roadmap for choosing roadmaps. · **18** Nine months later, he has learned a lot. About roadmaps.
> **19** The job market has notes. · **20** Four hundred applications. Two replies. · **21** One rejection. And one rejection with better grammar. · **22** Entry level. Five years of experience required. · **23** For a job that didn't exist three years ago.
> **24** Then comes the interview question. · **25** Where do you see yourself in five years? · **26** Employed, hopefully. Alive, ideally.
> **27** His feed says everyone else figured it out. · **28** A seventeen-year-old just raised a million dollars. · **29** His cousin did a bootcamp and now earns more than his father. · **30** So at dinner, his parents ask. Very gently. · **31** So what exactly is it that you do?
> **32** Here's what nobody tells him. · **33** The technology isn't the problem. The syllabus is.
> **34** So Rohan stops waiting for permission. · **35** He builds one small thing. Then one more. · **36** Not for marks. Not for the resume. · **37** Because the future isn't going to be handed to him. · **38** It has to be built.
> **39** The syllabus is outdated. · **40** He isn't.

```json
  "instructions": "Add the marker-doodle theme tokens with illustration-specific tokens.",
  "content": "// Design tokens — Marker Doodle\n// Copied to <project>/video/src/data/theme.ts by `video style <name> --set marker-doodle`.\n\nexport const THEME = {\n  id: \"marker-doodle\",\n  name: \"Marker Doodle\",\n\n  colors: {\n    background: \"#F7F1E3\", // warm paper\n    surface: \"#FFFDF6\", // clean sheet on the desk\n    surfaceAlt: \"#EDE3CB\", // aged paper / shadow block\n    text: \"#141414\", // marker black\n    textSecondary: \"#6F675A\", // pencil grey\n    accent: \"#FFD400\", // highlighter yellow\n    accentAlt: \"#FF4B3E\", // red pen\n    accentBlue: \"#2050FF\", // ballpoint blue (screens/UI)\n    accentSoft: \"rgba(255, 212, 0, 0.35)\",\n    line: \"rgba(20, 20, 20, 0.18)\",\n  },\n\n  fonts: {\n    heading: \"Impact, 'Arial Black', 'Helvetica Neue', sans-serif\",\n    body: \"'Helvetica Neue', Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif\",\n    mono: \"'SF Mono', Consolas, 'Courier New', monospace\",\n    headingWeight: 900,\n    bodyWeight: 700,\n    headingLetterSpacing: \"0.005em\",\n    lineHeights: { heading: 0.94, body: 1.2 },\n  },\n\n  motion: {\n    // Marker snap: arrives instantly, lands hard, barely settles.\n    easing: [0.19, 1, 0.22, 1] as const,\n    durations: { enter: 12, exit: 8, hold: 26 },\n    stagger: 3,\n    spring: { damping: 15, stiffness: 220, mass: 0.7 },\n    maxAnimationsPerScene: 5,\n  },\n\n  layout: {\n    safeMargin: 96,\n    maxTextWidth: 1180,\n    align: \"left\" as const,\n  },\n\n  treatment: {\n    shadow: \"8px 8px 0 #141414\", // hard offset, no blur\n    border: \"4px solid #141414\",\n    radius: 2,\n    grain: false,\n    gradient: false,\n  },\n\n  // Illustration-specific tokens (read by the SVG scenes).\n  illustration: {\n    stroke: 7, // standard ink line @1920x1080\n    strokeBold: 11, // hero outline\n    strokeHair: 3.5, // detail lines\n    dash: \"14 10\", // doodle dashed guides\n    wobble: 1.6, // px of hand-drawn irregularity\n    dotGrid: \"rgba(20, 20, 20, 0.085)\",\n  },\n} as const;\n\nexport type Theme = typeof THEME;\n"
}
```

**🛠 Write File**

````json
{
  "path": "video-design-types/marker-doodle/README.md",
  "instructions": "Write the design language guide for the marker-doodle style.",
  "content": "# Marker Doodle\n\nA notebook margin come to life: warm paper, thick black marker lines, a\nhighlighter that argues with the sentence, and a red pen that grades it.\nFunny, human, deliberately imperfect — every line looks drawn by a person in a\nhurry, then animated with perfect comic timing.\n\n## Palette\n\n| Token | Value | Use |\n|---|---|---|\n| background | `#F7F1E3` | Warm paper — never pure white |\n| surface | `#FFFDF6` | Cards, letters, screens |\n| surfaceAlt | `#EDE3CB` | Aged paper, shadow blocks, stacks |\n| text | `#141414` | Marker ink |\n| textSecondary | `#6F675A` | Pencil labels, captions |\n| accent | `#FFD400` | Highlighter — behind ONE keyword per scene |\n| accentAlt | `#FF4B3E` | Red pen — stamps, cross-outs, corrections |\n| accentBlue | `#2050FF` | Ballpoint — browser/UI chrome only |\n| line | `rgba(20,20,20,0.18)` | Ruled lines, connection lines |\n\nRules:\n- Paper is the stage. Never fill the whole frame with a dark color; let the\n  paper breathe and use ink density for contrast.\n- Yellow is a highlighter, not a fill: it goes BEHIND text, wipes on after the\n  word lands, and overlaps the letters slightly.\n- Red is the punchline pen: one dramatic stamp or cross-out per scene, max.\n- Blue only for \"technology\" surfaces (browser, chat, job portal).\n\n## Typography\n\n- Display: Impact / Arial Black, uppercase, 900 — hero words at 120–220px,\n  stacked with line-height 0.94 so lines nearly collide.\n- Body: Helvetica 700, 34–44px, sentence case.\n- Mono: SF Mono / Consolas for UI text, counters, timestamps, form fields.\n- Rotate display type −2° to +2° occasionally; let it feel pasted on.\n- Keywords get the highlighter block; punchlines get the red pen.\n\n## Illustration\n\n- Thick black outlines (`stroke` 7, `strokeBold` 11) with round caps and joins.\n- Every path is slightly off — hand-drawn, never ruler-straight. Add gentle\n  kinks to long lines.\n- Fill with paper tones only, plus flat yellow/red/blue accents. No gradients.\n- Character \"Rohan\": swoopy side-parted hair, round glasses, hoodie, permanent\n  under-eye bags. Drawn with 12–20 paths — a doodle, not a portrait.\n- Recurring props: sticky notes, browser tabs, stamped envelopes, roadmaps,\n  red rejection letters, a battered syllabus book.\n\n## Motion\n\n- **Draw-on**: paths reveal with `strokeDashoffset` over 10–14 frames, hand\n  speed, staggered by 2–3 frames. This is the signature move.\n- **Highlighter wipe**: block scales X 0→1 in 6–8 frames, slightly overshooting\n  the word, landing after the word.\n- **Stamp**: scale 1.8→1 in 5 frames with 2–3px shake, plus a 3-frame paper\n  jolt; the whole frame can nudge 4px on impact.\n- **Paste**: sticky notes spring in from off-frame with 4–7° rotation.\n- **Cross-out**: two quick strokes, 5 frames each, drawn after the word is read.\n- Exits are hard cuts or a 6-frame vertical slam. Never a soft fade to black.\n\n## Scene composition (1920×1080)\n\n- Two-zone layout: type column (120–900px) and illustration zone\n  (960–1820px); flip sides every 2–3 scenes to keep rhythm.\n- One idea per scene, one keyword per sentence. If two jokes compete, split.\n- Keep the paper edge visible: drawings can bleed off-frame, type cannot.\n- Number/label every scene like a notebook page: mono caption top-left\n  (`SUBJECT 001`, `FIG. 04`) — it sells the scrapbook feel and hides cuts.\n\n## Example scene shape\n\n```\n[AUDIO]: \"Entry level. Five years of experience required.\"\n→ Job card pastes in (spring, −3°): \"ENTRY LEVEL\" in mono, big.\n  Under it, \"5+ YEARS EXPERIENCE\" SLAMS in as a red stamp, shaking the card.\n  A tiny door labeled \"ENTRY\" is drawn too small for the stick figure.\n  Hold. Red circle scribbles around \"5+ YEARS\".\n```\n"
}
````

```json
{
  "path": "projects/engineering-student/design.md",
  "instructions": "Record the design decisions, palette, type and motion language.",
  "content": "# Design — engineering-student\n\n**Style:** `marker-doodle` (new style, created in `video-design-types/marker-doodle/`)\n\n## Mood\n\nA smart friend explaining your life on the back of a notebook. Funny, fast and\nwarm, never mean — the jokes target the system, not the student. The paper\ntexture keeps it human; the red pen does the punching.\n\n## Palette\n\n- Paper `#F7F1E3`, sheets `#FFFDF6`, aged paper `#EDE3CB`\n- Ink `#141414`, pencil `#6F675A`\n- Highlighter `#FFD400` — one keyword per scene, always behind the word\n- Red pen `#FF4B3E` — stamps, cross-outs, corrections, punchlines\n- Ballpoint `#2050FF` — browser/phone/UI chrome only\n\n## Typography\n\nTwo families: Impact/Arial Black for display (uppercase, 900, tight 0.94\nline-height) and Helvetica 700 for spoken-caption body text. SF Mono/Consolas\nfor UI labels, counters, timers, page captions. Heavy weights only — no light\ntype anywhere.\n\n## Layout\n\n16:9 two-zone composition: type column on one side (safe margin 96px), doodle\non the other, alternating sides every 2–3 scenes. Mono page captions\n`FIG. 01`–`FIG. 30` top-left sell the notebook feel and cover the cuts.\n\n## Motion\n\nDraw-on is the signature: every illustration is stroke-revealed with dash\noffsets (10–14f, 2–3f stagger). Highlighter wipes land after the word, stamps\nslam in 5f and jolt the frame 4px, sticky notes spring in with rotation,\ncross-outs are two quick red strokes. Exits are hard cuts, never fades.\n\n## Sound/edit rhythm\n\nAudio at 1.1× with a dry, deadpan delivery. Punchlines get their own line and\ntheir own scene; the edit never adds a beat the narrator doesn't have. No music\nbed, no SFX — the voice is the track, the drawings keep time.\n\n## Per-scene\n\nSee `storyboard.md` for the exact 30 beats, frame timings and on-screen text.\n"
}
```

```
export const THEME = {
  id: "marker-doodle",
  name: "Marker Doodle",

  colors: {
    background: "#F7F1E3", // warm paper
    surface: "#FFFDF6", // clean sheet on the desk
    surfaceAlt: "#EDE3CB", // aged paper / shadow block
    text: "#141414", // marker black
    textSecondary: "#6F675A", // pencil grey
    accent: "#FFD400", // highlighter yellow
    accentAlt: "#FF4B3E", // red pen
    accentBlue: "#2050FF", // ballpoint blue (screens/UI)
    accentSoft: "rgba(255, 212, 0, 0.35)",
    line: "rgba(20, 20, 20, 0.18)",
  },

  fonts: {
    heading: "Impact, 'Arial Black', 'Helvetica Neue', sans-serif",
    body: "'Helvetica Neue', Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif",
    mono: "'SF Mono', Consolas, 'Courier New', monospace",
    headingWeight: 900,
    bodyWeight: 700,
    headingLetterSpacing: "0.005em",
    lineHeights: { heading: 0.94, body: 1.2 },
  },

  motion: {
    // Marker snap: arrives instantly, lands hard, barely settles.
    easing: [0.19, 1, 0.22, 1] as const,
    durations: { enter: 12, exit: 8, hold: 26 },
    stagger: 3,
    spring: { damping: 15, stiffness: 220, mass: 0.7 },
    maxAnimationsPerScene: 5,
  },

  layout: {
    safeMargin: 96,
    maxTextWidth: 1180,
    align: "left" as const,
  },

  treatment: {
    shadow: "8px 8px 0 #141414", // hard offset, no blur
    border: "4px solid #141414",
    radius: 2,
    grain: false,
    gradient: false,
  },

  // Illustration-specific tokens (read by the SVG scenes).
  illustration: {
    stroke: 7, // standard ink line @1920x1080
    strokeBold: 11, // hero outline
    strokeHair: 3.5, // detail lines
    dash: "14 10", // doodle dashed guides
    wobble: 1.6, // px of hand-drawn irregularity
    dotGrid: "rgba(20, 20, 20, 0.085)",
  },
} as const;

export type Theme = typeof THEME;
```

>   const frame=useCurrentFrame();
>   const p=interpolate(frame,[delay,delay+dur],[0,1],{extrapolateLeft:'clamp',extrapolateRight:'clamp',easing:ease});
>   const fillP=interpolate(frame,[delay+dur*0.5,delay+dur],[0,1],{...});
>   return <path d={d} fill={fill ?? "none"} fillOpacity={fill?fillP:undefined} stroke={color} strokeWidth={w} strokeLinecap="round" strokeLinejoin="round" pathLength={1} strokeDasharray={dash ? THEME.illustration.dash : "1"} strokeDashoffset={dash ? undefined : 1-p} opacity={opacity} />;
> };
> ```

> `Arrow`: props d, delay, head? Use Ink plus arrowhead at end point. Need end coordinates; accept `to:[number,number]` and compute angle from last segment? Simpler: `ArrowLine` with `from`, `to`, `curve` (0 = straight), draws quadratic curve and arrowhead at `to` using computed angle. Compute control point perpendicular offset, and arrowhead direction approximated from control→to.
> 
> Implementation:
> ```
> const [x1,y1]=from,[x2,y2]=to;
> const mx=(x1+x2)/2, my=(y1+y2)/2;
> const dx=x2-x1, dy=y2-y1; const len=Math.hypot(dx,dy);
> const nx=-dy/len, ny=dx/len;
> const cx=mx+nx*curve, cy=my+ny*curve;
> const d=`M${x1} ${y1} Q${cx} ${cy} ${x2} ${y2}`;
> const angle=Math.atan2(y2-cy,x2-cx);
> const h=14;
> head paths: points at x2 - h*cos(angle±0.5), ...
> ```
> Then two Ink paths for head with delay+dur-4. Good.
> 
> `Check`: small path with delay.
> 
> `Cross`: two Ink paths; API x,y,size,delay,color.

> _Reasoning_
> Bugs:
> 1. L28 calls `useCurrentFrame()` inside `stack` helper defined in the component body and called in a loop? `const stack = (at) => interpolate(useCurrentFrame(), ...)` — hooks called inside map loops. That's a rules-of-hooks violation. Move `useCurrentFrame()` to a const `frame` at top and use it in stack. Also the background opacity calls useCurrentFrame inline (fine, one call). Fix: `const frame = useCurrentFrame(); const stack = (at) => interpolate(frame, ...)`.
> 
> 2. L29 uses `useCurrentFrame()` inside a `.map()` for dust — violation. Move to `const frame = useCurrentFrame()` and use frame.
> 
> 3. L24: `<PermissionSlip delay={0} />` twice; each contains a Sheet (absolute) inside a wrapper. The wrappers both at left 480 top 180, and clipPath. Since Sheet is absolute at 0,0 within a non-positioned wrapper? The wrapper is position absolute, so Sheet absolute 0,0. Good. But the `clipPath` on the wrapper clips at its own bounding box, which is 0 size (children absolute). Clip-path percentages refer to the element's box; with no width/height, it clips everything. Need the wrapper to have explicit width 880 height 500. Add width/height to the wrapper. Also the halves should be at same position; both wrappers at 480,180 with width 880. Good.
> - The `PermissionSlip` Sheet has box-shadow and transform scale; fine.
> - PENDING stamp at 1060,200 is outside the slip (slip 480-1360; stamp at 1060 within).
> - Tear line at 925 from 160 to 720; okay.
> - Rohan at 1180,560 with rise; but the slip is at 480-1360 y 180-680, so Rohan overlaps the slip's right side. He should appear after the slip tears/falls. At f42+ the halves drop; Rohan rises. Fine, but at f0-30 he is visible behind/over the slip. The wrapper is rendered after the stamps? Rohan is after the tear SVG, so on top of the slip. Move Rohan's opacity to appear only after f36. Add opacity interpolate [34,42].
> - "NOT REQUIRED." at 96,780 may overlap Rohan at 1180+, no.
> 
> 4. L27: `handDown + leave` — hand starts at -260, moves to 0, then leaves to -520. But `leave` starts at f54 from 0 to -520. Good.
> - The giant hand arm is a rect from y -40 to 260 with fill AGED, plus palm below. Box at 500-740. The arm/palm coordinate 1040-1300; text at left 96-800. Good.
> - Rohan's hand: arm line from 560,1080 to 620,760 with w 26; palm path. `reach` [24,34] moves -180 (up), then `back` [42,48] +80. Fine.
> - The dashed outline at 1080,540 appears at f60. Good.
> 
> 5. L26: The sweep transform moves sheets by 900px and opacity 1+sweep. At f54 sweep 0, opacity 1; by 66 sweep -1 → opacity 0, moved off. Good. But `sweep` interpolates [54,66] from 0 to -1. At f70, still -1. Fine.
> - BUILT card pops at 64 while sheets still sweeping; okay.
> - X positions within sheets: Cross at (340,90) is relative to the Paper, not the Sheet! The Cross is inside Sheet's children, so it's positioned relative to the Sheet's padding box (absolute? Cross renders `<g>` with Ink paths; SVG? Cross returns a `<g>` intended for inside an SVG, but it's placed directly inside the HTML Sheet children! That's invalid: `<g>` outside an `<svg>` won't render in HTML? React will render an unknown SVG element in HTML namespace; browsers may not display it. In L14 I used CircleScribble directly in Paper, not inside Sheet. In L26 I put `<Cross>` inside `<Sheet>` children — that won't render properly.
> 
> Need to wrap Cross in an `<svg>` with absolute positioning inside the Sheet:
> ```
> <svg width={620} height={520} style={{position:'absolute',inset:0}}>
>   <Cross x={340} y={90} size={150} .../>
> </svg>
> ```
> But Sheet has padding 26; an absolute svg inset 0 covers padding box? `position:absolute` inside Sheet (which has no position:relative? Sheet style has position absolute, so it is a containing block). inset 0 → full sheet. Good. Add wrapper svg.
> 
> Similarly L23's `<Cross>` is inside a div that contains an `<svg>` separately — I placed `<Cross .../>` after `</svg>` inside the div, so it's an HTML `<g>` — won't render. Need to include Cross inside the svg. In L23, the book div has `<svg>...</svg>` then `<Cross .../>`. Fix by moving Cross inside the SVG (at the end before `</svg>`) with coordinates relative to 520x560.
> 
> Same for L29? No Cross. L30 no Cross.
> 
> L28 `Stamp` has x/y within Paper? Stamp is absolute relative to Paper (position absolute), and I placed it directly in Paper — fine.
> 
> L25 `Check` inside a div (HTML) — Check returns Ink (a `<path>`), also invalid outside SVG! In L25, `<Check .../>` is inside the project 01 div. Need an svg wrapper. Also L23 `Check` inside a div that has an `<svg>` but outside it — invalid. Wait L23 Check is placed after `</svg>` inside the tile div. Need to move inside.

Better: use `viewBox="0 0 300 344"` with `preserveAspectRatio` and `<Rohan scale={1}>`, then size the SVG in CSS to the desired size. The Rohan component scale is only needed to fit within the viewBox; just use scale 1 and set svg width = 300*desiredScale, height = 344*desiredScale.
